=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Given two integers, n and k, an alternating permutation is a permutation of the first n positive integers such that no two adjacent elements are both odd or both even.
Return the k-th alternating permutation sorted in lexicographical order. If there are fewer than k valid alternating permutations, return an empty list.
 
Example 1:

Input: n = 4, k = 6
Output: [3,4,1,2]
Explanation:
The lexicographically-sorted alternating permutations of [1, 2, 3, 4] are:

[1, 2, 3, 4]
[1, 4, 3, 2]
[2, 1, 4, 3]
[2, 3, 4, 1]
[3, 2, 1, 4]
[3, 4, 1, 2] ← 6th permutation
[4, 1, 2, 3]
[4, 3, 2, 1]

Since k = 6, we return [3, 4, 1, 2].

Example 2:

Input: n = 3, k = 2
Output: [3,2,1]
Explanation:
The lexicographically-sorted alternating permutations of [1, 2, 3] are:

[1, 2, 3]
[3, 2, 1] ← 2nd permutation

Since k = 2, we return [3, 2, 1].

Example 3:

Input: n = 2, k = 3
Output: []
Explanation:
The lexicographically-sorted alternating permutations of [1, 2] are:

[1, 2]
[2, 1]

There are only 2 alternating permutations, but k = 3, which is out of range. Thus, we return an empty list [].

 
Constraints:

1 <= n <= 100
1 <= k <= 10^15

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def permute(self, n: int, k: int) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's maybe derive formula for ways in terms of counts and start:
def count_ways(odd_left, even_left, start):
    total = odd_left + even_left
    if start == 1:
        odd_positions = (total + 1)//2
        even_positions = total//2
    else:
        odd_positions = total//2
        even_positions = (total + 1)//2
    if odd_left != odd_positions or even_left != even_positions:
        return 0
    val = fact[odd_left] * fact[even_left]
    return CAP if val > CAP else val

Need maybe if start is parity of first remaining position. For full sequence total_count = count_ways(O,E,0)+count_ways(O,E,1). For first candidate x, start = 1 - (x&1). Let's test if x odd, start even. If x even, start odd. Good.

Potential issue: If previous parity exists, and we iterate candidates, we can just check if x%2 == 1 - prev_parity. But if no previous, we don't check. However, if no previous and candidate parity p, count = count_ways(o_rem,e_rem,1-p). If count=0, skip. Good.

Need maybe maintain remaining odd/even counts:
odd_remaining = O
even_remaining = E
used = [False]*(n+1)
prev_parity = -1
for pos in range(n):
    for x in range(1,n+1):
        if used[x]: continue
        p = x & 1
        if prev_parity != -1 and p != 1 - prev_parity:
            continue
        # try choose x
        if p == 1:
            new_o = odd_remaining - 1
            new_e = even_remaining
        else:
            new_o = odd_remaining
            new_e = even_remaining - 1
        cnt = count_ways(new_o, new_e, 1 - p)
        if k > cnt:
            k -= cnt
        else:
            result.append(x)
            used[x] = True
            odd_remaining, even_remaining = new_o, new_e
            prev_parity = p
            break
    else:
        return [] # should not happen if k valid
return result

Let's maybe write code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def permute(self, n: int, k: int) -> List[int]:
        CAP = 10**15
        fact = [1]*(n+1)
        for i in range(1, n+1):
            fact[i] = min(CAP, fact[i-1]*i)
        
        def ways(odd_left: int, even_left: int, start: int) -> int:
            total = odd_left + even_left
            if start == 1:
                need_odd = (total + 1)//2
                need_even = total//2
            else:
                need_odd = total//2
                need_even = (total + 1)//2
            if odd_left != need_odd or even_left != need_even:
                return 0
            val = fact[odd_left] * fact[even_left]
            return CAP if val > CAP else val
        
        odd_total = (n + 1)//2
        even_total = n//2
        total = ways(odd_total, even_total, 0) + ways(odd_total, even_total, 1)
        if total > CAP:
            total = CAP
        if k > total:
            return []
        
        used = [False]*(n+1)
        ans = []
        odd_rem = odd_total
        even_rem = even_total
        prev_parity = -1
        
        for _ in range(n):
            chosen = -1
            for x in range(1, n+1):
                if used[x]:
                    continue
                p = x & 1
                if prev_parity != -1 and p != 1 - prev_parity:
                    continue
                if p == 1:
                    new_o = odd_rem - 1
                    new_e = even_rem
                else:
                    new_o = odd_rem
                    new_e = even_rem - 1
                cnt = ways(new_o, new_e, 1 - p)
                if k > cnt:
                    k -= cnt
                else:
                    chosen = x
                    break
            if chosen == -1:
                return []
            ans.append(chosen)
            used[chosen] = True
            if chosen & 1:
                odd_rem -= 1
            else:
                even_rem -= 1
            prev_parity = chosen & 1
        
        return ans

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def permute(self, n: int, k: int) -> List[int]:
        CAP = 10**15

        fact = [1] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            fact[i] = min(CAP, fact[i - 1] * i)

        def ways(odd_left: int, even_left: int, start: int) -> int:
            # start: 0 = even, 1 = odd
            total = odd_left + even_left

            if start == 1:  # starts with odd
                need_odd = (total + 1) // 2
                need_even = total // 2
            else:           # starts with even
                need_odd = total // 2
                need_even = (total + 1) // 2

            if odd_left != need_odd or even_left != need_even:
                return 0

            val = fact[odd_left] * fact[even_left]
            return CAP if val > CAP else val

        odd_total = (n + 1) // 2
        even_total = n // 2

        total = ways(odd_total, even_total, 0) + ways(odd_total, even_total, 1)
        total = min(CAP, total)

        if k > total:
            return []

        used = [False] * (n + 1)
        ans = []

        odd_rem = odd_total
        even_rem = even_total
        prev_parity = -1  # -1 means no previous element

        for _ in range(n):
            chosen = -1

            for x in range(1, n + 1):
                if used[x]:
                    continue

                p = x & 1

                if prev_parity != -1 and p != 1 - prev_parity:
                    continue

                if p == 1:
                    new_odd = odd_rem - 1
                    new_even = even_rem
                else:
                    new_odd = odd_rem
                    new_even = even_rem - 1

                cnt = ways(new_odd, new_even, 1 - p)

                if k > cnt:
                    k -= cnt
                else:
                    chosen = x
                    break

            if chosen == -1:
                return []

            ans.append(chosen)
            used[chosen] = True

            if chosen & 1:
                odd_rem -= 1
            else:
                even_rem -= 1

            prev_parity = chosen & 1

        return ans
```
